Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. Um... well, I hope I'm in the right place for this, and it's probably not a huge deal, but I've just been dealing with this... I guess you could call it a constant restless feeling? 

It's really not the worst thing in the world, I just feel like I'm wound a little too tight, if that makes sense. Like I just can't fully settle. I'll be at work—I'm a retail buyer—and I'm supposed to be calmly sitting through a long planning meeting, or even at home just trying to do something relaxing like painting or doing yoga, and I just... I can't quite relax into it. I always feel like I need to be moving. 

Is that the sort of thing people usually come here for? I hope I'm explaining it okay.

So thankfully it's not affecting my sleep at all. Is that normal? To feel restless during the day but still sleep okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive thoughts or urges to do something over and over?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no... I don't think so. I don't really get stuck on specific thoughts like that, and I don't have any urges to repeat things or anything like that. It's really just that physical feeling of needing to shift around or get up when I'm supposed to be sitting still. \n\nI hope I'm answering that right—is that what you meant by that question? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're experiencing this restless feeling?\n\nPatient: No, not really anxious. I mean, maybe just a tiny bit frustrated with myself sometimes because I know I should be able to just sit quietly like everyone else, but I wouldn't call it anxiety. It's more of a physical energy, like a motor running, rather than a worried feeling in my head. \n\nI'm generally a pretty happy, positive person! I don't feel nervous or stressed out, just... fidgety, I guess. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not confusing things by saying it's not anxiety.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no, I don't think so. I really try to be responsible that way. Is it okay that I don't have that issue? I mean, I might toss and turn a tiny bit when I first get into bed, but once I'm out, I'm out. It's really just during the day when I'm awake and supposed to be, you know, sitting still. So thankfully it's not affecting my sleep at all. Is that normal? To feel restless during the day but still sleep okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive thoughts or urges to do something over and over?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no... I don't think so. I don't really get stuck on specific thoughts like that, and I don't have any urges to repeat things or anything like that. It's really just that physical feeling of needing to shift around or get up when I'm supposed to be sitting still. \n\nI hope I'm answering that right—is that what you meant by that question? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're experiencing this restless feeling?\n\nPatient: No, not really anxious. I mean, maybe just a tiny bit frustrated with myself sometimes because I know I should be able to just sit quietly like everyone else, but I wouldn't call it anxiety. It's more of a physical energy, like a motor running, rather than a worried feeling in my head. \n\nI'm generally a pretty happy, positive person! I don't feel nervous or stressed out, just... fidgety, I guess. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not confusing things by saying it's not anxiety.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no, I don't think so. I actually try really hard to be careful about what I do. Working as a retail buyer, you know, I have to make a lot of decisions, so I've learned to weigh my options pretty well. \n\nI mean, sure, I might grab a coffee or a magazine at the checkout line on a whim every now and then, but for anything important, I definitely think it through. I really try to be responsible that way. Is it okay that I don't have that issue? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need for your notes.\n\nDoctor: Are there any particular places or situations where this restless feeling is more pronounced?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it happens a bit at work, and maybe at home too. It's not terrible, I promise! Just... well, at work, sometimes I get a little ahead of myself when I'm feeling that way, and it's led to a few corrected purchase orders. My planning coordinator just has to double-check my things a little more often now. I feel so bad about making extra work for her, but she's very sweet about it. \n\nAnd at home, um, during family dinners... sometimes I guess I can be a little much. I just get so eager to jump into the conversation, and my family has mentioned that they feel talked over. It makes things a little tense sometimes, which I hate, because I really just want everyone to have a nice time. \n\nI'm trying really hard to work on both of those things, though! Do you think that's the kind of thing you were looking for? I hope I'm not sounding too difficult to be around!\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble engaging in leisure activities quietly?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I have trouble with that. I mean, I actually really enjoy my downtime! I like to read, or maybe do a little gardening on the weekends. \n\nI suppose I do like to have some music playing or maybe the TV on in the background while I do things, but I think a lot of people do that, right? It's just nice to have a little ambiance. I definitely don't feel like I *can't* be quiet if I need to be. I hope that's an okay answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble paying attention to what you're doing when you're feeling restless?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I don't think so. Um... even if I'm feeling a little fidgety or restless, I can still focus perfectly fine on whatever I'm doing. With my job, looking at all those spreadsheets and catalogs, I really have to pay close attention to the details, and I pride myself on doing a good job with that. \n\nSo, no, I wouldn't say the restlessness really distracts me or makes me lose focus. I hope it's alright that I'm saying no to that one? I just want to make sure I'm being helpful and giving you the right information for your assessment.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're able to complete tasks from start to finish without getting distracted by your restlessness?\n\nPatient: Oh, yes, absolutely. Um... I don't really have a problem finishing things once I start them. Oh, well, I guess that kind of ties right back into what I was just mentioning a second ago! Um... I don't know if I'd say I *need* to be active all the time, because that makes it sound a bit... intense, maybe? But I do just naturally move from one task right to the next.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... talking excessively? I don't... I don't think so. I mean, I certainly hope I don't dominate conversations! That sounds so rude, and I always try my best to be a good listener. 

I suppose sometimes, if I'm really passionate about a new product line at work or, you know, just feeling really engaged in a topic, I might get a little enthusiastic. My friends sometimes joke that I can talk a mile a minute when I'm excited, but I think that's just because I want to share things with them, you know? It's coming from a good place. I certainly try to make sure everyone else gets a turn to speak. 

I really try to be mindful of other people's feelings. Does that... I mean, is it normal to get a bit chatty when you're enthusiastic? I just want to make sure I'm not coming across as overbearing or anything. Am I explaining that alright?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... triggered by emotions? I don't really think so. I mean, I wouldn't say my feelings make me restless in a bad way. 

I guess if I'm really excited about something—like finding a great new vendor for the store—I might have a little extra spring in my step, but isn't that just normal enthusiasm? And if I'm frustrated, maybe I'll just channel that into cleaning out a closet or something productive instead of sitting around dwelling on it. But I don't feel like I'm losing control or anything like that. I just like to turn my feelings into action, if that makes sense? 

I really hope I'm not making it sound like my emotions are all over the place. I try very hard to stay level-headed. Is that the kind of thing you were looking for? I can try to think of a better example if that wasn't quite right.

Oh, gosh, interrupting? I really, really try not to do that. I know how frustrating it is when someone talks over you, so I make a conscious effort to be polite. 

I mean, I suppose... well, sometimes at work, if I overhear the junior buyers discussing a vendor or a product line, I might sort of... jump in? I really hope they don't think I'm being rude or intrusive when I do that. Do you think that counts as interrupting? I just want to make sure I'm answering your question properly, I don't want you to think I'm just barging into people's conversations all the time!

Oh, disorganized? No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, my job requires a lot of attention to detail, so I really try my best to keep things orderly.

So thankfully it's not affecting my sleep at all. Is that normal? To feel restless during the day but still sleep okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive thoughts or urges to do something over and over?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no... I don't think so. I don't really get stuck on specific thoughts like that, and I don't have any urges to repeat things or anything like that. It's really just that physical feeling of needing to shift around or get up when I'm supposed to be sitting still. \n\nI hope I'm answering that right—is that what you meant by that question? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're experiencing this restless feeling?\n\nPatient: No, not really anxious. I mean, maybe just a tiny bit frustrated with myself sometimes because I know I should be able to just sit quietly like everyone else, but I wouldn't call it anxiety. It's more of a physical energy, like a motor running, rather than a worried feeling in my head. \n\nI'm generally a pretty happy, positive person! I don't feel nervous or stressed out, just... fidgety, I guess. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not confusing things by saying it's not anxiety.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no, I don't think so. I actually try really hard to be careful about what I do. Working as a retail buyer, you know, I have to make a lot of decisions, so I've learned to weigh my options pretty well. \n\nI mean, sure, I might grab a coffee or a magazine at the checkout line on a whim every now and then, but for anything important, I definitely think it through. I really try to be responsible that way. Is it okay that I don't have that issue? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need for your notes.\n\nDoctor: Are there any particular places or situations where this restless feeling is more pronounced?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it happens a bit at work, and maybe at home too. It's not terrible, I promise! Just... well, at work, sometimes I get a little ahead of myself when I'm feeling that way, and it's led to a few corrected purchase orders. My planning coordinator just has to double-check my things a little more often now. I feel so bad about making extra work for her, but she's very sweet about it. \n\nAnd at home, um, during family dinners... sometimes I guess I can be a little much. I just get so eager to jump into the conversation, and my family has mentioned that they feel talked over. It makes things a little tense sometimes, which I hate, because I really just want everyone to have a nice time. \n\nI'm trying really hard to work on both of those things, though! Do you think that's the kind of thing you were looking for? I hope I'm not sounding too difficult to be around!\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble engaging in leisure activities quietly?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I have trouble with that. I mean, I actually really enjoy my downtime! I like to read, or maybe do a little gardening on the weekends. \n\nI suppose I do like to have some music playing or maybe the TV on in the background while I do things, but I think a lot of people do that, right? It's just nice to have a little ambiance. I definitely don't feel like I *can't* be quiet if I need to be. I hope that's an okay answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble paying attention to what you're doing when you're feeling restless?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I don't think so. Um... even if I'm feeling a little fidgety or restless, I can still focus perfectly fine on whatever I'm doing. With my job, looking at all those spreadsheets and catalogs, I really have to pay close attention to the details, and I pride myself on doing a good job with that. \n\nSo, no, I wouldn't say the restlessness really distracts me or makes me lose focus. I hope it's alright that I'm saying no to that one? I just want to make sure I'm being helpful and giving you the right information for your assessment.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're able to complete tasks from start to finish without getting distracted by your restlessness?\n\nPatient: Oh, yes, absolutely. Um... I don't really have a problem finishing things once I start them. Like I mentioned with my work, I really have to see things all the way through to the end, from reviewing the catalogs to finalizing the orders. \n\nEven if I'm feeling a little... you know, energetic or fidgety, I always make sure I get my work done. I really pride myself on being reliable! I would hate to leave my team hanging or leave a project half-finished just because I felt a little restless. So, yes, I can definitely complete my tasks from start to finish. \n\nI hope I'm being helpful by saying that? I just want to make sure I'm answering correctly and not messing up your evaluation!\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel restless when you're doing something you find boring?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, boring things? Well, I suppose nobody really *likes* being bored, do they? But I wouldn't say I get noticeably restless. \n\nI mean, if I'm stuck in a long meeting or doing some really tedious paperwork, I might shift in my seat a little bit or doodle on my notepad, but... I think that's just a normal way to pass the time, isn't it? I certainly don't get up and pace around or anything disruptive like that. I always try to be polite and professional, even if the task isn't the most exciting. \n\nI really try to just push through and get it done. Is that... is that the kind of thing you're looking for? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of examples.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have more energy than your peers?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I suppose I might? But I don't think it's in a bad way or anything! I just... I really like to stay productive. \n\nLike, even on the weekends, I tend to finish one thing and immediately start another. I'll get the laundry going, and then check some emails, maybe reorganize some of the product samples I brought home from the office, and then go touch up a painting I've been working on. I just like making the most of my day! My husband does say that I never really power down, but... well, I think it's just being efficient, right? \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound frantic or anything. I just like to be helpful and keep things moving. Is that... does that answer your question okay? I want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you need to be doing something active all the time?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, I guess that kind of ties right back into what I was just mentioning a second ago! Um... I don't know if I'd say I *need* to be active all the time, because that makes it sound a bit... intense, maybe? But I do just naturally move from one task right to the next. \n\nLike I was saying about my weekends, finishing the laundry, jumping right to my emails, sorting those product samples, and then touching up a painting... I just really like to keep busy. When my husband says I never really power down, I think he just means I'm very dedicated to keeping our home and my work in order. It's a good thing to be productive, don't you think? \n\nI hope it's alright that I used the same example again! I just want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture without sounding like I'm bouncing off the walls or anything. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking excessively or dominating conversations when you're feeling restless?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... talking excessively? I don't... I don't think so. I mean, I certainly hope I don't dominate conversations! That sounds so rude, and I always try my best to be a good listener. \n\nI suppose sometimes, if I'm really passionate about a new product line at work or, you know, just feeling really engaged in a topic, I might get a little enthusiastic. My friends sometimes joke that I can talk a mile a minute when I'm excited, but I think that's just because I want to share things with them, you know? It's coming from a good place. I certainly try to make sure everyone else gets a turn to speak. \n\nI really try to be mindful of other people's feelings. Does that... I mean, is it normal to get a bit chatty when you're enthusiastic? I just want to make sure I'm not coming across as overbearing or anything. I don't really think so. But I don't feel like I'm losing control or anything like that. I just like to turn my feelings into action, if that makes sense? \n\nI really hope I'm not making it sound like my emotions are all over the place. I try very hard to stay level-headed. Is that the kind of thing you were looking for? I'm definitely not trying to take over their conversation or make them feel bad. Oh, blurting out answers? Um... I really try to be a good listener, I promise. I know it's important to let people finish their thoughts. 

I guess... well, sometimes during vendor negotiations, or when I'm doing my STEM mentoring sessions with the younger girls, I might get a little too eager? I just get excited to share what I know. Does that make sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound like a terrible conversation partner. I really do try to wait my turn most of the time!

I just want to make sure I'm being helpful and giving you the right information for your assessment.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're able to complete tasks from start to finish without getting distracted by your restlessness?\n\nPatient: Oh, yes, absolutely. Um... I don't really have a problem finishing things once I start them. Like I mentioned with my work, I really have to see things all the way through to the end, from reviewing the catalogs to finalizing the orders. \n\nEven if I'm feeling a little... you know, energetic or fidgety, I always make sure I get my work done. I really pride myself on being reliable! I would hate to leave my team hanging or leave a project half-finished just because I felt a little restless. So, yes, I can definitely complete my tasks from start to finish. \n\nI hope I'm being helpful by saying that? I want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you need to be doing something active all the time?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, I guess that kind of ties right back into what I was just mentioning a second ago! I really pride myself on being reliable! I would hate to leave my team hanging or leave a project half-finished just because I felt a little restless. So, yes, I can definitely complete my tasks from start to finish. \n\nI hope I'm being helpful by saying that? I just want to make sure I'm answering correctly and not messing up your evaluation!\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel restless when you're doing something you find boring?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, boring things? Well, I suppose nobody really *likes* being bored, do they? But I wouldn't say I get noticeably restless. \n\nI mean, if I'm stuck in a long meeting or doing some really tedious paperwork, I might shift in my seat a little bit or doodle on my notepad, but... I think that's just a normal way to pass the time, isn't it? I certainly don't get up and pace around or anything disruptive like that. I always try to be polite and professional, even if the task isn't the most exciting. \n\nI really try to just push through and get it done. Is that... is that the kind of thing you're looking for? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of examples.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have more energy than your peers?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I suppose I might? My husband does say that I never really power down, but... well, I think it's just being efficient, right? \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound frantic or anything. I just like to be helpful and keep things moving. Is that... does that answer your question okay? I want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you need to be doing something active all the time?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, I guess that kind of ties right back into what I was just mentioning a second ago! I just want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture without sounding like I'm bouncing off the walls or anything. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking excessively or dominating conversations when you're feeling restless?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... talking excessively? I don't... I don't think so. I mean, I certainly hope I don't dominate conversations! My friends sometimes joke that I can talk a mile a minute when I'm excited, but I think that's just because I want to share things with them, you know? It's coming from a good place. I certainly try to make sure everyone else gets a turn to speak. \n\nI really try to be mindful of other people's feelings. Does that... ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, a need to always be doing something? Um... I wouldn't call it a *need*, necessarily. I just really enjoy being productive! I think it's good to make the most of your time, don't you? 

Like, even on weekends, I do tend to finish one thing and then just sort of... immediately start another. I guess it happens a bit at work, and maybe at home too. It's not terrible, I promise! Just... well, at work, sometimes I get a little ahead of myself when I'm feeling that way, and it's led to a few corrected purchase orders. My planning coordinator just has to double-check my things a little more often now. I feel so bad about making extra work for her, but she's very sweet about it. \n\nAnd at home, um, during family dinners... sometimes I guess I can be a little much. I just get so eager to jump into the conversation, and my family has mentioned that they feel talked over. It makes things a little tense sometimes, which I hate, because I really just want everyone to have a nice time. \n\nI'm trying really hard to work on both of those things, though! Do you think that's the kind of thing you were looking for? I hope I'm not sounding too difficult to be around!\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble engaging in leisure activities quietly?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I have trouble with that. I mean, I actually really enjoy my downtime! I like to read, or maybe do a little gardening on the weekends. \n\nI suppose I do like to have some music playing or maybe the TV on in the background while I do things, but I think a lot of people do that, right? It's just nice to have a little ambiance. I definitely don't feel like I *can't* be quiet if I need to be. I hope that's an okay answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble paying attention to what you're doing when you're feeling restless?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I don't think so. Um... even if I'm feeling a little fidgety or restless, I can still focus perfectly fine on whatever I'm doing. With my job, looking at all those spreadsheets and catalogs, I really have to pay close attention to the details, and I pride myself on doing a good job with that. \n\nSo, no, I wouldn't say the restlessness really distracts me or makes me lose focus. I hope it's alright that I'm saying no to that one? I just want to make sure I'm being helpful and giving you the right information for your assessment.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're able to complete tasks from start to finish without getting distracted by your restlessness?\n\nPatient: Oh, yes, absolutely. Um... I don't really have a problem finishing things once I start them. Like I mentioned with my work, I really have to see things all the way through to the end, from reviewing the catalogs to finalizing the orders. \n\nEven if I'm feeling a little... you know, energetic or fidgety, I always make sure I get my work done. I really pride myself on being reliable! I would hate to leave my team hanging or leave a project half-finished just because I felt a little restless. So, yes, I can definitely complete my tasks from start to finish. \n\nI hope I'm being helpful by saying that? I just want to make sure I'm answering correctly and not messing up your evaluation!\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel restless when you're doing something you find boring?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, boring things? Well, I suppose nobody really *likes* being bored, do they? But I wouldn't say I get noticeably restless. \n\nI mean, if I'm stuck in a long meeting or doing some really tedious paperwork, I might shift in my seat a little bit or doodle on my notepad, but... I think that's just a normal way to pass the time, isn't it? I certainly don't get up and pace around or anything disruptive like that. I always try to be polite and professional, even if the task isn't the most exciting. \n\nI really try to just push through and get it done. Is that... is that the kind of thing you're looking for? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of examples.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have more energy than your peers?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I suppose I might? But I don't think it's in a bad way or anything! I just... I really like to stay productive. \n\nLike, even on the weekends, I tend to finish one thing and immediately start another. I'll get the laundry going, and then check some emails, maybe reorganize some of the product samples I brought home from the office, and then go touch up a painting I've been working on. I just like making the most of my day! My husband does say that I never really power down, but... well, I think it's just being efficient, right? \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound frantic or anything. I just like to be helpful and keep things moving. Is that... does that answer your question okay? I want to make sure I'm giving you what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you need to be doing something active all the time?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, I guess that kind of ties right back into what I was just mentioning a second ago! But I do just naturally move from one task right to the next. \n\nLike I was saying about my weekends, finishing the laundry, jumping right to my emails, sorting those product samples, and then touching up a painting... I just really like to keep busy. When my husband says I never really power down, I think he just means I'm very dedicated to keeping our home and my work in order. I just want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture without sounding like I'm bouncing off the walls or anything. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking excessively or dominating conversations when you're feeling restless?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... talking excessively? I don't... I don't think so. I mean, I certainly hope I don't dominate conversations! That sounds so rude, and I always try my best to be a good listener. \n\nI suppose sometimes, if I'm really passionate about a new product line at work or, you know, just feeling really engaged in a topic, I might get a little enthusiastic. My friends sometimes joke that I can talk a mile a minute when I'm excited, but I think that's just because I want to share things with them, you know? It's coming from a good place. I mean, is it normal to get a bit chatty when you're enthusiastic? I just want to make sure I'm not coming across as overbearing or anything. Am I explaining that alright?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your restlessness is ever triggered by strong emotions, such as excitement or frustration?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... triggered by emotions? I don't really think so. I mean, I wouldn't say my feelings make me restless in a bad way. \n\nI guess if I'm really excited about something—like finding a great new vendor for the store—I might have a little extra spring in my step, but isn't that just normal enthusiasm? And if I'm frustrated, maybe I'll just channel that into cleaning out a closet or something productive instead of sitting around dwelling on it. But I don't feel like I'm losing control or anything like that. I just like to turn my feelings into action, if that makes sense? \n\nI really hope I'm not making it sound like my emotions are all over the place. I try very hard to stay level-headed. Is that the kind of thing you were looking for? I can try to think of a better example if that wasn't quite right.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh, interrupting? I really, really try not to do that. I know how frustrating it is when someone talks over you, so I make a conscious effort to be polite. \n\nI mean, I suppose... well, sometimes at work, if I overhear the junior buyers discussing a vendor or a product line, I might sort of... jump in? Just to correct a little detail or offer my opinion before they've actually asked for it. But I'm only doing it because I want to support them and make sure they have the right information, you know? I'm definitely not trying to take over their conversation or make them feel bad. It's just that I have so much experience, and I want to be helpful. \n\nI really hope they don't think I'm being rude or intrusive when I do that. Do you think that counts as interrupting? I just want to make sure I'm answering your question properly, I don't want you to think I'm just barging into people's conversations all the time!\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel disorganized or have trouble keeping your workspace or living space tidy?\n\nPatient: Oh, disorganized? No, I wouldn't say that. Um... I really try to be a good listener, I promise. Like, I'll hear where they're going with a question and I just want to be helpful and give them the answer right away, so I might sort of jump in before they're totally done. I really pride myself on being patient with people. \n\nSo, no, I don't feel that sort of restless urge where I just can't bear to wait my turn. I hope that clarifies things? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture, because I really do try to be as polite as possible!\n\nDoctor: Do you feel the need to always be doing something when you're not working, even during leisure time?\n\nPatient: Oh, a need to always be doing something?
